<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Cash Value of Life Insurance</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company maintains an aggregate of approximately $2.9&nbsp;million of life insurance on a former
stockholder of EWC. The Company is the designated beneficiary, and there is no restriction on the
use of proceeds. At December&nbsp;31, 2008, there was $2.9&nbsp;million of loans outstanding on the
insurance policies, and the cash surrender value has been pledged by the Company to secure certain
debt.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Goodwill</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company follows the provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;142, <I>Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets</I>, which
requires that goodwill and intangible assets acquired in a purchase business combination and
determined to have an indefinite useful life are not amortized, but instead are tested for
impairment on an annual basis or more frequently if certain indicators arise. Goodwill represents
the excess of purchase price of an acquired business over the fair value of the underlying net
tangible and intangible assets. The Company operates in one segment and goodwill is evaluated at
the company level as there is only one reporting unit. Goodwill is evaluated in the fourth fiscal
quarter of each year.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Debt Issuance Costs</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Amortization of debt issuance costs is computed by using the straight-line method, which
approximates the interest method, over the life of the related debt.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Asset Retirement Obligations</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;143, <I>Accounting for Asset Retirement Obligations, </I>and FASB
Interpretation No. (&#147;FIN&#148;) 47, <I>Accounting for Conditional Asset Retirement Obligations, </I>the Company
recognizes liabilities associated with the eventual retirement of tangible long-lived assets
whenever legal obligations will eventually require that we dismantle the asset and restore the
property to its original state. The Company recognizes an estimated liability for future costs
associated with the retirement of underground storage tanks. A liability for the fair value of an
asset retirement obligation and a corresponding increase to the carrying value of the related
long-lived asset are recorded at the time an underground tank is put into service, a facility is
purchased with underground tanks or a lease is entered into that requires the Company to return the
property to its original condition. The increase in carrying value is included in property, plant
and equipment on the consolidated balance sheets. The Company amortizes the amount added to the
property, plant and equipment and recognizes accretion expense in connection with the discounted
liability over the remaining estimated economic lives of the tanks.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Additionally, the Company recognizes an estimated liability for future costs when leased property
requires the Company to return the property back to its original condition upon termination of the
lease. A liability for the fair value of an asset retirement obligation and a corresponding
increase to the carrying value of the related long-lived asset are recorded at the time the lease
is entered into. The increase in carrying value is included in property, plant and equipment on the
consolidated balance sheets. The Company amortizes the amount added to the property, plant and
equipment and recognizes accretion expense in connection with the discounted liability over the
life of the lease.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company&#146;s estimated asset retirement obligation is based on estimated economic lives, estimates
as to the cost to dismantle the property in the future, and federal and state regulatory
requirements. The liability is discounted using an interest rate at the time the liability is
incurred. Increased liabilities as a resulting from revisions of estimated cash flows is discounted
using an interest rate at the time of the revision.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-42-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Stock Options</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Effective January&nbsp;1, 2006, the Company adopted the fair value method of accounting for awards of
employee stock and options in accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;123, <I>Accounting for Stock-Based
Compensation</I>, as amended by SFAS No.&nbsp;148, <I>Accounting for Stock-Based Compensation, Transition and
Disclosure</I>, and SFAS No.&nbsp;123R, <I>Share-Based Payment</I>. These standards require the cost associated
with employee services in exchange for equity instruments based on the grant-date fair value of the
award, be recognized over the period during which the employee is required to provide services in
exchange for the award. No compensation cost is recognized for awards for which employees do not
render the requisite service. Because the Company changed its method of accounting from the
intrinsic method as per Accounting Principles Board (&#147;APB&#148;) Opinion No.&nbsp;25, upon adoption, the
grant date fair value of employee share options was estimated using the Black-Scholes model.
Compensation cost for the unvested portion of equity awards granted prior to January&nbsp;1, 2006, will
be recognized over the remaining vesting periods. Due to the prospective adoption of SFAS No.
123R, results of operations for prior periods have not been restated.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Revenue Recognition</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company derives its revenues primarily from the operation of unattended fueling sites and the
distribution of fuel, lubricants, chemicals and other related products. Revenues from the sale of
bulk products are recognized when persuasive evidence of an arrangement exists, the price is
determinable, the product has been delivered, the title has been transferred to the customer and
collectibility is reasonably assured. Revenues from sale of fuel at unattended fueling sites are
recognized when the fuel is dispensed to the customer.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Taxes are assessed by various governmental authorities on many different types of transactions. The
Company accounts for taxes collected from customers and remitted to governmental authorities in
accordance with Emerging Issues Task Force (&#147;EITF&#148;) No.&nbsp;06-3, <I>How Taxes Collected From Customers
and Remitted to Governmental Authorities Should Be Presented in the Income Statement (That Is,
Gross Versus Net Presentation)</I>. The Company presents taxes on a net basis in its consolidated
statement of operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Income Taxes</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company accounts for income taxes under SFAS No.&nbsp;109, <I>Accounting for Income Taxes</I>, and FIN 48,
<I>Accounting for Uncertainty in Income Taxes &#151; an Interpretation of FASB Statement No.&nbsp;109</I>.
Accordingly, the Company provides deferred income tax assets and liabilities based on the estimated
future tax effects of differences between the financial and tax basis of assets and liabilities
based on currently enacted tax laws. The tax balances and income tax expense recognized by us are
based on management&#146;s interpretation of the tax laws of multiple jurisdictions. Income tax expense
also reflects management&#146;s best estimates and assumptions regarding, among other things, the level
of future taxable income and tax planning. Future changes in tax laws, changes in projected levels
of taxable income, tax planning, and adoption and implementation of new accounting standards could
impact the effective tax rate and tax balances recorded by us.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Basic and Diluted Net Income (Loss) Per Share</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Net income (loss)&nbsp;per share is computed in accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;128, <I>Earnings per Share</I>. Basic
net income (loss)&nbsp;per share is based upon the weighted average number of common shares outstanding
during the period. Diluted net income or loss per share is based upon the weighted average number
of common shares outstanding and dilutive common stock equivalents outstanding during the period.
Common stock equivalents are options granted and shares of restricted stock issued under the
Company&#146;s stock awards plans and are calculated under the treasury stock method. Common equivalent
shares from unexercised stock options and restricted stock are excluded from the computation when
there is a loss as their effect is antidilutive, or if the exercise price of such options is
greater than the average market price of the stock for the period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Environmental Costs</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company is subject to federal, state and local environmental laws and regulations.
Environmental expenditures are expensed or capitalized depending on their future economic benefit.
Expenditures that relate to an existing condition caused by past operations and that have no future
economic benefits are expensed. Ongoing
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-43-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">environmental compliance costs, including maintenance and monitoring costs, are expensed as
incurred. Liabilities for expenditures of a noncapital nature are recorded when environmental
assessment and/or remediation is probable, and the costs can be reasonably estimated. Such amounts
are immaterial for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007, and 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Loss Contingencies</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company accounts for contingent losses in accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;5, <I>Accounting for
Contingencies</I>. Under SFAS No.&nbsp;5, loss contingency provisions are recorded for probable losses at
management&#146;s best estimate of a loss, or when a best estimate cannot be made, a minimum loss
contingency amount is recorded. These estimates are often initially developed substantially
earlier than the ultimate loss is known, and the estimates are refined each accounting period, as
additional information is known.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Financial Instruments</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Management believes the carrying value of the Company&#146;s cash and cash equivalents, receivables,
inventories, accounts payable and notes payable approximate fair value due to the short maturity of
the instruments. Management believes the carrying value of the Company&#146;s long-term debt
approximates fair value because the related borrowing rate approximates the rate the Company would
pay for new debt with similar terms.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Reclassifications</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Certain reclassifications have been made to prior period financial statements to conform to the
presentation adopted in the current year.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 2 &#151; RECENTLY ISSUED ACCOUNTING PRONOUNCEMENTS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In July&nbsp;2006, the Financial Accounting Standards Board (&#147;FASB&#148;) issued FIN 48, <I>Accounting for
Uncertainty in Income Taxes-An Interpretation of FASB Statement No.&nbsp;109</I>, which is effective for
fiscal years beginning after December&nbsp;15, 2006. This interpretation clarifies the accounting for
uncertainty in income taxes recognized in an enterprise&#146;s financial statements by prescribing a
recognition threshold and measurement attribute for the financial statement recognition and
measurement of a tax position taken or expected to be taken in a tax return. This interpretation
also provides guidance on derecognition, classification, interest and penalties, accounting in
interim periods, disclosure and transition. Any transition adjustments will be recorded directly
to the beginning balance of retained earnings in the period of adoption and reported as a change in
accounting principle in the accompanying financial statements. The adoption of FIN 48 did not have
a material impact on the Company&#146;s financial position, results of operations or cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In September&nbsp;2006, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;157, <I>Fair Value Measurements</I>. This statement defines
fair value, establishes a framework for measuring fair value in generally accepted accounting
principles and expands disclosures about fair value measurements. SFAS No.&nbsp;157 is effective for
fiscal years beginning after November&nbsp;15, 2007. The FASB has since issued FASB Staff Positions
(&#147;FSP&#148;) FAS 157-1 and 157-2, and proposed FSP FAS 157-c to amend SFAS No.&nbsp;157. FSP FAS 157-1
clarifies the exclusion of SFAS No.&nbsp;13, <I>Accounting for Leases</I>, and its related interpretive
accounting pronouncements that address leasing transactions from the scope of SFAS No.&nbsp;157, while
FSP FAS 157-2 delays for one year the effective date of SFAS No.&nbsp;157 for all nonfinancial assets
and nonfinancial liabilities, except those that are recognized or disclosed at fair value in the
financial statements on a recurring basis. FSP FAS 157-c clarifies the principles in SFAS No.&nbsp;157
on the fair value measurement of liabilities. Effective January&nbsp;1, 2008, the Company adopted SFAS
No.&nbsp;157, which defines fair value as the exchange price that would be received for an asset or paid
to transfer a liability (an exit price) in the principal or most advantageous market for the asset
or liability in an orderly transaction between market participants at the measurement date. SFAS
No.&nbsp;157 establishes a three-level fair value hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs used to measure
fair value. This hierarchy requires entities to maximize the use of observable inputs and minimize
the use of unobservable inputs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In October&nbsp;2008, the FASB issued FSP FAS 157-3 to further amend SFAS No.&nbsp;157. This staff position
addresses the issue of determining fair value when the market for that asset is not active and
modified the example related to Level 3 of inputs. The Company&#146;s adoption of SFAS No.&nbsp;157 did not
have an impact on the Company&#146;s consolidated
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-44-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">financial statements. The Company is currently evaluating the impact of SFAS No.&nbsp;157 for
nonfinancial assets and nonfinancial liabilities on its consolidated financial statements, which
will become effective on January&nbsp;1, 2009.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In February&nbsp;2007, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;159, <I>The Fair Value Option for Financial Assets and
Financial Liabilities-Including an amendment of FASB Statement No.&nbsp;115</I>, which permits an entity to
choose to measure financial instruments and certain other items similar to financial instruments at
fair value. All subsequent changes in fair value for the financial instruments would be reported in
earnings. By electing the fair value option, an entity can also achieve consistent accounting for
related assets and liabilities without having to apply complex hedge accounting. SFAS No.&nbsp;159
became effective January&nbsp;1, 2008. The adoption of SFAS No.&nbsp;159 did not have a material impact on
the Company&#146;s consolidated financial position, results of operations or cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In December&nbsp;2007, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;160, <I>Noncontrolling Interests in Consolidated Financial</I>
<I>
Statements &#151; an Amendment of ARB No.&nbsp;51</I>. SFAS No.&nbsp;160 establishes principles and requirements on
how to treat the portion of equity in a subsidiary that is not attributable directly or indirectly
to a parent. This is commonly known as a minority interest. The objective of SFAS No.&nbsp;160 is to
improve relevance, comparability, and transparency concerning ownership interests in subsidiaries
held by parties other than the parent by providing disclosures that clearly distinguish between
interests of the parent and interests of the noncontrolling owners as well as the related impacts
on the consolidated statement of income and the consolidated statement of financial position. SFAS
No.&nbsp;160 also provides guidance on disclosures related to changes in the parent&#146;s ownership interest
and deconsolidation of a subsidiary. SFAS No.&nbsp;160 is effective for fiscal years, and interim
periods within those fiscal years, beginning on or after December&nbsp;15, 2008. The Company does not
believe that the adoption of SFAS No.&nbsp;160 will have a material impact on its financial position,
results of operations or cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In December&nbsp;2007, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;141(R), <I>Business Combinations</I>. SFAS No.&nbsp;141(R)
establishes principles and requirements on how the acquirer in a business combination recognizes
and measures in its financial statements the identifiable assets acquired, the liabilities assumed
and any noncontrolling interest in the entity acquired. In addition, SFAS No.&nbsp;141(R) provides
guidance on the recognition and measurement of goodwill acquired in the business combination or a
gain from a bargain purchase as well as what information to disclose to enable users of the
financial statements to evaluate the nature and financial impact of the business combination. SFAS
No.&nbsp;141(R) requires contingent consideration to be recognized in earnings until settled. SFAS No.
141(R) also requires acquisition-related transaction and restructuring costs to be expensed rather
than treated as part of the cost of the acquisition. The provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;141(R) apply
prospectively to business combinations for which the acquisition date is on or after the beginning
of the first annual reporting period beginning on or after December&nbsp;15, 2008. The Company is
currently evaluating the impact SFAS No.&nbsp;141(R) will have on its financial position, results of
operations and cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In March&nbsp;2008, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;161, <I>Disclosures about Derivative Instruments and Hedging
Activities, an Amendment to FASB Statement No.&nbsp;133</I>. This statement is intended to improve financial
reporting about derivative instruments and hedging activities by requiring enhanced qualitative and
quantitative disclosures regarding derivative instruments, gains and losses on such instruments and
their effects on an entity&#146;s financial position, financial performance and cash flows. SFAS No.&nbsp;161
is effective for financial statements issued for fiscal years beginning after November&nbsp;15, 2008,
and interim periods within those fiscal years. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company does not have
any derivative instruments and does not expect SFAS No.&nbsp;161 to have a material impact on the
Company&#146;s results from operations or financial position.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In April&nbsp;2008, the FASB issued FSP FAS 142-3, <I>Determination of the Useful Life of Intangible
Assets</I>. FSP FAS 142-3 amends the factors that should be considered in developing a renewal or
extension assumptions used for purposes of determining the useful life of a recognized intangible
asset under FASB Statement No.&nbsp;142, <I>Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets</I>. FSP FAS 142-3 is intended
to improve the consistency between the useful life of a recognized intangible asset under SFAS No.
142 and the period of expected cash flows used to measure the fair value of the asset under SFAS
No.&nbsp;141(R) and other U.S. generally accepted accounting principles. FSP FAS 142-3 is effective for
fiscal years beginning after December&nbsp;15, 2008. Early application is not permitted. The Company
does not believe the impact of adopting FSP FAS 142-3 will have a material effect on the Company&#146;s
financial position or results of operations.
</DIV>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In May&nbsp;2008, the FASB issued SFAS No.&nbsp;162, <I>The Hierarchy of Generally Accepted Accounting
Principles</I>. SFAS No.&nbsp;162 identifies the sources of accounting principles and provides entities with
a framework for selecting the principles used in preparation of financial statements that are
presented in conformity with generally accepted accounting principles. The adoption of SFAS No.&nbsp;162
is not expected to have a material impact on the Company&#146;s financial position.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In June&nbsp;2008, the FASB ratified EITF Issue No.&nbsp;07-5, <I>Determining Whether an Instrument (or Embedded
Feature) is Indexed to an Entity&#146;s Own Stock</I>. This issue is effective for financial statements
issued for fiscal years beginning after December&nbsp;15, 2008, and interim periods within those fiscal
years. Early application is not permitted. Paragraph 11(a) of SFAS No.&nbsp;133, <I>Accounting for
Derivatives and Hedging Activities</I>, specified that a contract that would otherwise meet the
definition of a derivative but is both (a)&nbsp;indexed to the Company&#146;s own stock and (b)&nbsp;classified in
stockholders&#146; equity in the statement of financial position would not be considered a derivative
financial instrument. EITF Issue No.&nbsp;07-5 provides a new two-step model to be applied in
determining whether a financial instrument or an embedded feature is indexed to an issuer&#146;s own
stock and thus able to qualify for the SFAS No.&nbsp;133 paragraph 11(a) scope exception. The adoption
of this statement will not have a material impact on the Company&#146;s consolidated financial position,
results of operations and cash flows.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 3 &#151; ACQUISITION AND DIVESTITURE OF PROPANE ASSETS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Prior to July&nbsp;2007, the Company had a propane business line that operated out of multiple locations
in Texas and New Mexico and was fully integrated with the fuel and
lubricant distribution businesses.
Effective July&nbsp;1, 2007, the Company acquired all of the assets of Propane Direct, LLC, an Oklahoma
distributor of propane. This acquisition enhanced the Company&#146;s presence in the propane
distribution arena, in terms of wholesale, commercial and residential customers. The total
purchase price was $2.4&nbsp;million plus inventory and net receivables of $0.3&nbsp;million. The cost was
financed through the acquisition of term loans.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On December&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company entered into and closed an Asset Purchase Agreement (&#147;Purchase
Agreement&#148;), by and between the Company and Propane Direct Enterprises, LLC, a Texas limited
liability company. Pursuant to the Purchase Agreement, the Company agreed to sell substantially
all of the assets of its propane distribution business to Propane Direct Enterprises, LLC,
excluding a $3.4&nbsp;million portion of the accounts receivable that are being retained by the Company,
for gross proceeds of $9.8&nbsp;million. The purchase price was paid by the delivery of three
promissory notes: (i)&nbsp;a short term unsecured promissory note for $7.7&nbsp;million; (ii)&nbsp;a subordinated
unsecured promissory note for approximately $1.5&nbsp;million with a termination date of January&nbsp;31,
2014 and bearing an annual interest rate of 3% over 12-month Libor; and (iii)&nbsp;a deed of trust note
for $0.6&nbsp;million with a termination date of January&nbsp;31, 2014 and bearing an annual interest rate of
4% over 12-month Libor. Subsequent to December&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company received full payment of the
promissory note for $7.7&nbsp;million in January&nbsp;2009 and received monthly payments on the later two
notes in accordance with the notes receivable agreements. Propane Direct Enterprises, LLC is a
business newly established by the Vice Chairman of the Company&#146;s board of
directors. See Note 13 for a discussion of related party transactions. Propane Direct
Enterprises, LLC is not related to Propane Direct, LLC, the Oklahoma limited liability company from
whom the Company acquired certain propane-related assets in July&nbsp;2007 as described above.
</DIV>

sale (in thousands):

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Since the operations and cash flows of the propane distribution business could not be clearly
distinguished, operationally and for financial reporting purposes as required by paragraph 41 of
SFAS No.&nbsp;144, the gain on the sale
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-46-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">of the propane business line of $1.9&nbsp;million was included in other income, net in the statement of
operations for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 4 &#151; ACQUISITIONS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In accordance with SFAS No.&nbsp;141, &#147;Business Combinations,&#148; the Company records assets acquired and
liabilities assumed at the date of acquisition at their respective fair values as of the
acquisition date in the consolidated balance sheet. The determination of fair value requires
management to make assumptions and estimates. The excess of the purchase price over the fair value
of the acquired net assets is recorded as goodwill. Results of operations from the date of
acquisition are recorded in the consolidated statement of operations.
</DIV>

24,641,276 shares of common stock. CFS operates 19 cardlock locations, as well as four cardlock

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company&#146;s original revolving line of credit (&#147;Revolver&#148;) provided for borrowing of the lesser
of $55.0&nbsp;million or a borrowing base, as defined, and was collateralized by accounts receivable,
inventory, cash value of life insurance policies, rolling stock and a limited personal guarantee by
a stockholder of the Company. Interest was payable monthly at prime plus 0.5% or LIBOR plus 3.0%,
at the Company&#146;s option. The Revolver was scheduled to mature on September&nbsp;30, 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In March&nbsp;2007 the Revolver was amended to, among other things, (a)&nbsp;extend the maturity to September
30, 2012, (b)&nbsp;increase the line of credit to $70.0&nbsp;million, (c)&nbsp;reduce the interest rate to LIBOR
plus 1.75% or prime, at the Company&#146;s option, for 2007 and thereafter based on levels of fixed
charge coverage, (d)&nbsp;provide for improved advance rates on certain collateral, (e)&nbsp;eliminate a
financial covenant to provide more flexibility for borrowing, and (f)&nbsp;release the personal
guarantee of the shareholder.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In September&nbsp;2007, and in connection with the Company&#146;s restatement of its consolidated financial
statements for December&nbsp;31, 2006, and March&nbsp;31, 2007, the Revolver was amended. The amendment
provided for (i)&nbsp;an increase in the LIBOR interest rate option to LIBOR plus 2.25% until November
1, 2007, and thereafter based on levels of fixed charge coverage, (ii)&nbsp;the Company to remain in
compliance with financial covenants and financial reporting covenants, and (iii)&nbsp;a waiver of any
event of default related to representations and warranties made with respect to financial
statements previously submitted to the bank as a result of the restatement.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In addition to the amendment to the Revolver, the lender also provided for certain term loan
borrowings of up to $20.0&nbsp;million. An initial term loan of $5.0&nbsp;million, which was advanced at
closing, and a $1.0&nbsp;million term loan, which was subsequently funded, are secured by certain
existing assets, including certain assets which secured three previously outstanding term notes,
which had balances of approximately $2.0&nbsp;million that were paid off from the $6.0&nbsp;million proceeds.
In addition, the lender also provided for an acquisition term loan line of credit in an aggregate
original principal amount of up to $14.0&nbsp;million. The acquisition term loans provide for certain
advance rates for subsequently purchased equipment and real estate based upon appraised values. The
term loans, which have a maturity of September&nbsp;30, 2012, are payable in monthly principal
installments based upon a 72-month amortization and bear interest at LIBOR plus 2.5% or prime plus
0.5%, at the Company&#146;s option, and are secured by the general assets of the Company.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On December&nbsp;28, 2007, the Company and the lender entered into a Third Amendment to the Revolver and
a Forbearance Agreement. The purpose of the Forbearance Agreement and Third Amendment was to, among
other things, provide: (i)&nbsp;a temporary $10.0&nbsp;million increase in the Company&#146;s revolving line of
credit to a total of $80.0&nbsp;million; (ii)&nbsp;an agreement on the part of the lender to forbear from
exercising its rights and remedies due to the Company&#146;s failure to comply with the fixed charge
coverage ratio financial covenant under the Second Amended and Restated Financing Agreement until
March&nbsp;7, 2008. In addition, the Forbearance Agreement and Third Amendment also created new
information requirements and a new financial covenant for the Company in consideration of the
lender&#146;s forbearance. In lieu of the Company&#146;s compliance with the fixed charge coverage ratio
financial covenant, during the forbearance period the Company was required to maintain certain
minimum levels of EBITDA. In addition, during the forbearance period the Company was required to
provide the lender with certain reports, projections and management&#146;s analysis of the Company&#146;s
financial position, daily borrowing base certifications, and weekly cash flow projections.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">As consideration for the Forbearance Agreement and Third Amendment, the Company agreed to pay the
lender aggregate fees of $50,000 for the temporary increase in the Company&#146;s revolving line of
credit. In addition, in consideration of the lender&#146;s forbearance, the Company agreed to pay
additional interest of 60 basis points (0.6%) on borrowings during the forbearance period.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On March&nbsp;28, 2008, the Company entered into a Fourth Amendment to the Revolver which replaced the
forbearance agreement and waived the previous events of default. Under the Fourth Amendment, the
amount provided for in the
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-50-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Revolver remained at $80.0&nbsp;million subject to an availability block of
$7.5&nbsp;million. The amount available for term loans was reduced from $20.0&nbsp;million to $9.0&nbsp;million.
In addition, the Fourth Amendment provides for the ability to increase the Revolver in the
agreement from $89&nbsp;million to $125&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On April&nbsp;14, 2008, the Company issued a promissory note for up to $5.0&nbsp;million to the Greinke
Personal Living Trust, Frank P. Greinke, Trustee, a related party, to be used for general working
capital purposes. The note was payable upon demand by the lender upon one business day prior
written notice. Interest on the note was at a rate of LIBOR (2.72%) plus 3.5%. Loans related to the
note were made during the second quarter of 2008 in the total amount of $6.5&nbsp;million. The note was
repaid in full on July&nbsp;23, 2008.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On April&nbsp;18, 2008, the Company entered into a Fifth Amendment to the Revolver whereby the lender
increased the credit commitment by a total of $2.7&nbsp;million. The increase was tied to a sale within
sixty days of a life insurance policy owned by the Company on a former Company executive. At the
time of the amendment, the insurance policy had a current cash surrender value of $2.9&nbsp;million. As
consideration for the amendment, the Company paid an amendment fee of $0.1&nbsp;million. On July&nbsp;5,
2008, the Company entered into a loan agreement with John Hancock Life Insurance Company whereby
the Company obtained a loan of $2.9&nbsp;million against the cash surrender value of the life insurance
policy. The lender approved the making of this loan and released the life insurance collateral from
the collateral held by the lender. The proceeds of the loan were used to reduce the revolving
credit loan from the lender.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On April&nbsp;30, 2008, the Company notified the lender that the Company had failed to meet the EBITDA
covenant required by the loan agreement and that the Company was in technical default of the
agreement. On July&nbsp;10, 2008, the Company entered into a Forbearance Agreement and Sixth Amendment
to the loan agreement with the lender whereby the lender agreed to forbear exercising its rights
under the loan agreement. The forbearance agreement included a provision whereby the Greinke
Personal Living Trust could join the lender as a secured lender in the amount of $5.0&nbsp;million. The
forbearance agreement also included a provision to allow the aggregate revolving line of credit
commitments to increase from $80.0&nbsp;million to $85.0&nbsp;million. As consideration for the forbearance
agreement, the interest rate during the forbearance period was increased by 200 basis points (2.0%)
as provided for in the loan agreement. On July&nbsp;23, 2008, the Greinke Personal Living Trust
contributed $5.0&nbsp;million to the lender and became a secured lender to the Company. As a result of
this contribution, the total commitments by the lender under the agreement increased from $80.0
million to $85.0&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On August&nbsp;8, 2008, the Company entered into a Seventh Amendment to the Second Amended and Restated
Financing Agreement which replaced the forbearance agreement and waived the previous event of
default. The Seventh Amendment also included other provisions including an increase in the interest
rates on the Revolver and the term loans, a reset of the EBITDA and fixed charge coverage ratio
covenant requirements, a reduction in the annual limit on capital expenditures from $5.0&nbsp;million to
$3.0&nbsp;million, a requirement for an equipment and real estate appraisal on all collateral supporting
the lender&#146;s term loans, a requirement that the Company reduce
its over-90&nbsp;day accounts receivable balance
to no greater than $4.5&nbsp;million by October&nbsp;31, 2008, accounts payable terms of no less than 30&nbsp;days
to a related party and its affiliates, and additional reporting requirements on an expanded and
more frequent basis. The Company was in compliance with the covenant requirements as of December
31, 2008. However, subsequent to December&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company&#146;s management requested that the
lenders reset the minimum EBITDA covenant so that it could avoid a technical default under the
covenants of its Revolver that would have occurred as a result of the Company&#146;s
operating losses caused by the severe downturn in the U.S. economy.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On March&nbsp;31, 2009, the Company and the lenders under its Second Amended and Restated Financing
Agreement dated March&nbsp;27, 2007, and The Greinke Personal Living Trust entered into an Eighth
Amendment. The Eighth Amendment to the Company&#146;s Second Amended and Restated Financing Agreement
decreases the total loan commitment of the lenders from
$85.0&nbsp;million to $50.0&nbsp;million, and lowers the
EBITDA covenant requirements for February&nbsp;2009 through April&nbsp;2009. As consideration for the
lenders&#146; agreement to the Eighth Amendment, the Company has agreed to an increase of 200 basis
points (2.0%) in the interest rates applicable to the Revolver and the term loans. The foregoing
description of the Eighth Amendment is not complete and is qualified in its entirety by reference
to the full text of the Eighth Amendment, a copy of which is filed herewith as Exhibit&nbsp;10.38 and
incorporated herein by reference.
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The 2004 term loan matures in November&nbsp;2009 and is payable in monthly principal installments of
$0.1&nbsp;million plus interest at prime (3.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2008) plus 1.5%. The term loan payable
is collateralized by a first lien on certain assets of United Fuel &#151; Texas, including but not
limited to, certain equipment, real estate and storage tanks. There are also cross default provisions
with the Company&#146;s Revolver, and certain financial covenants including maintenance of a specified
current ratio and net worth requirements. The term loan was paid in full on January&nbsp;5, 2009 from
proceeds of the sale of propane assets.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The 2005 term loan matures in October&nbsp;2010, payable in monthly principal payments of $15,000 plus
interest at prime (3.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2008) plus 1.5%. The note is collateralized by a first
lien on the property, plant and
equipment acquired in the Clark Oil acquisition. The loan is also guaranteed by the Company. The
loan was paid in full on January&nbsp;5, 2009 from proceeds of the sale of propane assets.
</DIV>


<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-51-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The note payable to a supplier is a Business Development Fund Agreement (&#147;BDF Agreement&#148;). The note
is unsecured, with scheduled retirements in annual amounts of $0.2&nbsp;million through December&nbsp;2008
and is subject to provisions of the BDF Agreement. The annual retirement is achieved by the Company
meeting minimum product purchasing volumes or paying shortfall rates for product volumes purchased
less than minimum levels prescribed in the BDF Agreement. Based on purchasing volumes, the balance
remaining at December&nbsp;31, 2008 is $0.1&nbsp;million.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The note payable to the shareholder of Reamax is payable in quarterly installments of principal and
interest of $0.1&nbsp;million, including interest at 10%. The note is scheduled to mature in April&nbsp;2010
with a final payment of $0.2&nbsp;million. The note is guaranteed by the Company.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Other notes payable are comprised primarily of notes for the purchase of vehicles, equipment and a
building. The notes have terms of four or five years, bear interest at rates ranging from 8.9%,
8.99%, or Wall Street Journal prime (3.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2008) plus 1.0% or 1.5%, with monthly
principal and interest payments aggregating approximately $50,000, and are collateralized by the
vehicles, equipment and building purchased.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 10 &#151; GOODWILL</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company follows the provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;142, <I>Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets</I>, which
requires that goodwill and intangible assets acquired in a purchase business combination and
determined to have an indefinite
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-53-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">useful life are not amortized, but instead are tested for impairment on an annual basis or more
frequently if certain indicators arise. Goodwill represents the excess of purchase price of an
acquired business over the fair value of the underlying net tangible and intangible assets. In the
second quarter of 2008, based on certain indicators, the Company tested goodwill for impairment and
recognized $0.3&nbsp;million in goodwill impairment and is included in general and administrative
expenses in the accompanying statement of operations. The Company performed its annual testing for
goodwill impairment in the fourth quarter of 2008 and concluded there was no additional impairment.
There was no impairment to goodwill for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2007 and 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 11 &#151; STOCKHOLDERS&#146; EQUITY</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>Preferred Stock</B>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Series&nbsp;A Cumulative Convertible Preferred Stock is entitled to receive 8% cumulative
preferential dividends based on the original purchase price, payable quarterly. For the one year
period beginning on the initial issuance date, as defined, the Company was permitted to pay the 8% dividend in
shares of the Company&#146;s common stock, based on the ten day average of the closing price immediately
prior to the dividend date. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, cash dividends aggregated $1.0
million of which $0.7&nbsp;million was paid in cash and $0.3&nbsp;million was accrued for at December&nbsp;31,
2008. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2007, cash dividends aggregated $0.8&nbsp;million, and 161,006
shares of common stock were issued in lieu of cash for a total of $80 per share. For the year
ended December&nbsp;31, 2006, cash dividends aggregated $0.5&nbsp;million, and 186,864 shares of common stock
were issued in lieu of cash, for an average cash equivalent of $57.60 per share.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">At the option of the holder, each share of preferred stock is convertible into shares of common
stock, determined by dividing $1,000 by the conversion price, as defined. During 2008, preferred
stock aggregating 300 shares was converted into 200,000 shares of the Company&#146;s common stock.
During 2007, preferred stock aggregating 365 shares was converted into 242,333 shares of the
Company&#146;s common stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">On or after March&nbsp;31, 2011, the Series&nbsp;A preferred stock is redeemable at the election of the
holders of two-thirds of the then outstanding Series&nbsp;A preferred stockholders, at a price equal to
125% of the original purchase price plus accrued dividends. The Company may redeem all or some of
the Series&nbsp;A preferred stock for cash at 100% of the original purchase price plus accrued dividends
if certain conditions are met, including: becoming listed on a national stock exchange; if the
Company&#146;s common stock trades for more than $3.00 per share for 25 out of 30 consecutive days; and
the Company has filed a registration statement relating to the common stock underlying the Series&nbsp;A
preferred stock. The holders of the Series&nbsp;A preferred stock also have a liquidation preference,
anti-dilution protection and special voting rights.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Shares of preferred stock were sold on March&nbsp;31, and April&nbsp;25, 2006, with a conversion price of
$1.50 per share. Because the fair value of the Company&#146;s common stock on those dates was $1.64 and
$1.75 per share, respectively, which was greater than the conversion price, the Company recorded a
one-time, non-cash deemed dividend of
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-54-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">approximately $1.6&nbsp;million pursuant to EITF Issue No.&nbsp;98-5 &#147;Accounting for Convertible Securities
with Beneficial Conversion Features or Contingently Adjustable Conversion Ratios.&#148;
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Financial covenants related to the Series&nbsp;A preferred stock include a limitation on the aggregate
amount of term debt incurred by the Company. The limitation on the term debt is defined as the
greater of $25&nbsp;million or 3.5 times adjusted trailing twelve months EBITDA, as defined, calculated
on a quarterly basis. At December&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company was in compliance with this covenant.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Equity Incentive Plan</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In February&nbsp;2005, the Company&#146;s majority stockholders and Board of Directors approved the 2005
Equity Incentive Plan (the &#147;2005 Plan&#148;). At the Company&#146;s Annual meeting of stockholders in June
2007, the stockholders approved an amendment to the 2005 Plan, which increased the number of shares
available under the plan to 3,000,000. The 2005 Plan provides for the granting of incentive stock
options, nonqualified stock options, restricted stock or stock bonuses, to employees, officers,
directors, consultants, independent contractors and advisors under certain conditions as defined in
the 2005 Plan. A total of 3,000,000 shares of common stock have been authorized and reserved for
issuance under the 2005 Plan, subject to certain adjustments to reflect changes in the Company&#146;s
capitalization. The 2005 Plan is to be administered by a committee appointed by the Board of
Directors, or if no committee has been formed, then by the Board of Directors. The committee, or
the Board of Directors, has the authority to interpret the 2005 Plan, to determine the persons to
whom the awards will be granted, to determine the basis upon which the awards will be granted, and
to determine the exercise price, duration and other terms of the awards to be granted under the
2005 Plan. As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, 700,726 shares of common stock remained available for grant
under the 2005 Plan.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><I>Stock Options. </I>Stock options are granted with an exercise price equal to the current market price
of our common stock at the grant date and have ten year contractual terms. Options typically vest
with one of the following schedules:
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 6pt">
<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt">

<TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="2%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left">a.</TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>One-third vesting annually over three years; or</TD>
</TR>

<TR>
    <TD style="font-size: 6pt">&nbsp;</TD>
</TR><TR valign="top" style="font-size: 10pt; color: #000000; background: transparent">
    <TD width="2%" style="background: transparent">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD width="3%" nowrap align="left">b.</TD>
    <TD width="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD>Quarterly in equal installments over three years, with the first vest date being the
quarter-end following the quarter of grant.</TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Certain options provide for accelerated vesting in the event of a Corporate Transaction as defined
in the option grant.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">During March&nbsp;2008, the Company granted an option to purchase up to 300,000 shares of the Company&#146;s
common stock. In November&nbsp;2008, in exchange for the cancellation of the March&nbsp;2008 option grant,
the Company granted an option to purchase up to 150,000 shares of the Company&#146;s common stock and a
restricted stock award of 150,000 shares (in the aggregate, the &#147;Modified Award&#148;). In accordance
with FAS 123R, the transaction was treated as a modification to the March&nbsp;2008 option grant and the
Company is required to recognize incremental compensation expense based on the difference between
the fair value of the Modified Award as compared to the March&nbsp;2008 grant as of the modification
date. The incremental cost of the Modified Award of $94,000 will be expensed over the term of the
Modified Award.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The weighted average grant date fair values of options granted during 2008, 2007 and 2006 was
$0.36, $0.67 and $0.32, respectively. There were no stock options exercised in 2008. As of December
31, 2008, the total unrecognized compensation cost related to stock options was $0.4&nbsp;million, which
is expected to be recognized over a weighted average period of 2.2&nbsp;years.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>Expected volatility: </B>Because of the lack of trading activity since the Company&#146;s stock became
publicly traded, the Company bases its estimates of stock price volatility on the five year
historical volatility of three similar companies&#146; common stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>Expected term: </B>Because of the lack of historical data regarding employees&#146; expected exercise
behavior, the Company utilizes a formula which is the sum of each of the vesting periods plus the
exercise period, divided by two. Options are assumed to be granted at the money.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>Risk-free interest rate: </B>The risk-free interest rate was based on the US Government Treasury yield
curve for periods equal to the expected term of the option as of the grant date.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><I>Restricted Stock Awards: </I>Restricted stock awards are typically granted with no exercise price.
Restricted stock awards granted to the Company&#146;s board of directors generally vest over one year.
Restricted stock awards granted to employees generally vest quarterly over three years with the
first vest date the quarter-end after the quarter of grant. A summary of the Company&#146;s unvested
restricted stock awards at December&nbsp;31, 2008 and changes during 2008 are presented below.

options exercised in fiscal 2008 and 2007 that would have resulted in a reclassification to reduce

financing activities.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 13 &#151; TRANSACTIONS WITH RELATED PARTIES</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company purchases bulk fuels with payment terms beyond the industry&#146;s standard from a company
in which the Company&#146;s Chairman of the Board owns a 100% interest. Billings from this company
aggregated approximately $130.4&nbsp;million and $48.0&nbsp;million for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 and
2007, respectively. Additionally, interest expense related to payments of these billings totaled
$0.5&nbsp;million for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 and no interest expense for the same period in
2007. The Company hauls bulk fuels for hire for this same company and billings to this company
aggregated $0.4&nbsp;million for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 and $25,000 during the period October
1, 2007, through December&nbsp;31, 2007. During 2008, the Company was dependent upon a company in which
the Company&#146;s Chairman of the Board owns a 100% interest to fund short-term working capital requirements.
Typically, these borrowings are repaid within 30&nbsp;days based on availability under our Revolver. As
of December&nbsp;31, 2008, we had $1.7&nbsp;million outstanding for
such borrowings, which amount is included in
accounts payable to related parties on the consolidated balance sheet.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In connection with the acquisition of CFS in October&nbsp;2007, the Company utilizes management and
accounting services from this same company. Under the terms of the Transition Services Agreement,
the Company was billed $1.1&nbsp;million for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008 and $0.2&nbsp;million during
the period October&nbsp;1, 2007, through December&nbsp;31, 2007. CFS leases real property on which its
cardlocks are located from another company in which the
Company&#146;s Chairman of the Board owns a 100%
interest. During the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, payments aggregated $0.5&nbsp;million and for the
period October&nbsp;1, 2007, through December&nbsp;31, 2007, payments aggregated $0.1&nbsp;million for such rents.
The Company relocated its corporate offices in September&nbsp;2008 and began renting facilities from the
same company that rents real property to CFS. Payments relating to the corporate offices aggregated
$39,000 for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008. The same company owns a residence that the Company
utilized during 2008 and 2007 for visiting executives. Payments aggregated $29,000 and $9,500 for
2008 and 2007, respectively. Additionally, for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, $43,000 of
interest was paid to the personal living
</DIV>

</DIV>

issued to the Greinke Personal Living Trust.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In February&nbsp;2006, the Company purchased an office building from the Company&#146;s Vice-Chairman and former President for
$0.7&nbsp;million, which served as its corporate headquarters. Prior to this, the Company leased office
space from him, as well as equipment. Payments for these lease arrangements aggregated
approximately $10,000 for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2006. The stockholder rented office space
from the Company, which approximated $10,000 for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2006. During 2008, the
Company sold the building to a company 100% owned by the Company&#146;s Vice-Chairman and former President for $0.7&nbsp;million.
The Company continued to rent space in the building through December&nbsp;2008 and paid rents
aggregating $10,000.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company purchases electricity from a company in which the Company&#146;s Vice-Chairman and former President has a 15%
interest. Payments for electricity to this company approximated $0.1&nbsp;million, $0.1&nbsp;million and
$0.2&nbsp;million for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007 and 2006, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company utilizes aviation services from two companies of which the Company&#146;s Vice-Chairman and former President owns
100% and 50% respectively. Payments to these two companies aggregated approximately $35,000, $0.2
million and $0.2&nbsp;million for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007 and 2006, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company sells fuel to two companies in which the Company&#146;s Vice-Chairman and former President
own interests of less than 10%. Sales aggregated $1.7&nbsp;million to these companies for each of the
years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007 and 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 14 &#151; CONCENTRATION OF CREDIT RISK</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Accounts receivable potentially subject the Company to concentrations of credit risk. The risk is
limited due to the large number of customer base and their dispersion across agricultural,
oilfield, and general commercial industries. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, no customer
accounted for 10% or more of the Company&#146;s consolidated revenues. The Company had no significant
concentrations of credit risk apart from customer operations occurring primarily in the
geographical regions of Texas, New Mexico, Oklahoma, Arizona and California. The Company maintains its
cash balances at several financial institutions located in those states, which at times may exceed
federally insured limits. These balances are insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation
up to $0.25&nbsp;million. The Company has not experienced any losses in such accounts and believes it is
not exposed to any significant credit risk.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, three suppliers furnished an aggregate of 49% of the
Company&#146;s purchases of product, 39% of which was from a related party. For the year ended December
31, 2007, three suppliers furnished an aggregate of 47% of the Company&#146;s purchases of product, 12%
of which was from a related party. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2006, two suppliers furnished
an aggregate of 40% of the Company&#146;s products for resale to customers. Management believes that, in
the unlikely event that these suppliers were unable to continue to provide this commodity,
alternative suppliers could be obtained without significant disruption to the Company&#146;s normal
operations.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>NOTE 15 &#151; EMPLOYEE BENEFIT PLANS</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>Defined Contribution Plan</B>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">The Company has a 401(k) plan for employees, which allows them to voluntarily contribute a
specified percentage of their salary on a pretax basis to various self-directed investments up to
the maximum federal limit. Prior to 2006, Company matching contributions were discretionary.
Effective January&nbsp;1, 2006, the Company matched 4% of employee contributions. Employees become
vested in Company matching contributions at 25% after two years, and 25% each year thereafter until
fully vested at 5&nbsp;years. Company contributions aggregated $0.5&nbsp;million, $0.4&nbsp;million and $0.2
million, for the years ended December&nbsp;31, 2008, 2007 and 2006, respectively.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-59-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;9. Changes in and Disagreements with Accountants on Accounting and Financial Disclosure.</B>
</DIV>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left">
<A name="116"></A>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;9A (T). Controls and Procedures.</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt"><B>(a)&nbsp;Disclosure Controls and Procedures</B>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Our management, including our chief executive officer and chief financial officer, evaluated the
effectiveness of our disclosure controls and procedures (as such term is defined in Rules&nbsp;13a-15(e)
or 15d-15(e) under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended (the &#147;Exchange Act&#148;) as December
31, 2008. Based on that evaluation, our management concluded that, as of the end of such period,
our disclosure controls and procedures were not effective because of certain material weaknesses
described below.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">A material weakness in internal control over financial reporting (as defined in paragraph 140 of
Auditing Standard No.&nbsp;2 of the Public Company Accounting Oversight Board) is a significant
deficiency, or combination of significant deficiencies, that results in more than a remote
likelihood that a material misstatement of the annual or interim consolidated financial statements
will not be prevented or detected. A significant deficiency is a control deficiency, or
combination of control deficiencies, that adversely affects a company&#146;s ability to initiate,
authorize, record, process, or report external financial data reliably in accordance with generally
accepted accounting principles (GAAP)&nbsp;such that there is more than a remote likelihood that a
misstatement of the company&#146;s annual or interim consolidated financial statements that is more than
inconsequential will not be prevented or detected.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">We identified a material weakness in the reconciliation of cash received from processors of credit
card transactions and the reconciliation of inventory related to those transactions. This material
weakness resulted in management&#146;s failure to prevent or detect errors in our consolidated financial
statements. As a result, we have restated our financial statements for the year ended December&nbsp;31,
2006, and the quarter ended March&nbsp;31, 2007.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">During 2008, we experienced significant turnover in the finance and accounting staff, including our
interim chief financial officer, a corporate controller, and several staff accountants. This
turnover resulted in considerable loss of knowledge related to our operations and accounting
processes and procedures. This change in finance and accounting staff impacted the routine
execution of accounting procedures and processes and resulted in a higher level of accounting
anomalies. In September&nbsp;2008, we hired a new chief financial officer and relocated our corporate
headquarters, including most accounting functions, from Texas to California. In October&nbsp;2008, we
hired several key accounting staff, including a corporate controller. These changes improved our
internal controls over financial reporting, but the controls were not in place for a sufficient
amount of time to remediate the material weakness as of
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-61-<!-- /Folio -->
</DIV>

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV style="font-family: 'Times New Roman',Times,serif">



<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">December&nbsp;31, 2008. We believe the change in staffing will be adequate to remediate this material
weakness by the first quarter of 2009.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">There was an internal control weakness surrounding our card lock inventory in locations outside of
California as of December&nbsp;31, 2008. We engage the services of third-party contractors to provide
physical inventory counts on several locations where we do not have automated tank gauges
installed. The physical inventory counts provided inconsistent readings and were not always
available at the end of each month. This internal control weakness resulted in fluctuations to our
inventory and cost of goods sold related to the specific sites and resulted in an increased
likelihood of potential material errors in our financial reporting.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">During 2008, we identified the following significant deficiencies, which were considered a material
weakness when aggregated. There was no formal purchasing system or asset disposal system to help
manage the addition or deletions of property and equipment, particularly with respect to the
acquisitions completed during 2007. There were no formal reviews of assets entered into the
corporate fixed asset management system, including inadequate review of new equipment purchases,
changes to the fixed assets utilized in our business operations, and no periodic review of physical
assets by location to the data maintained in the data files. These deficiencies increased the
likelihood of potential material errors in our financial reporting.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In light of the material weaknesses in our internal control over financial reporting described
above, we performed additional analysis and other post-closing procedures to ensure our
consolidated financial statements are prepared in accordance to GAAP. We added additional
management review processes to cash reconciliation procedures, accounts payable and accounts
receivable reconciliation procedures, and we strengthened the procedures in the inventory
reconciliation process in order to improve overall controls over card lock gross margins.
Accordingly, we believe that the consolidated financial statements included in the Form 10-K fairly
present in all material respects our financial position, results of operations and cash flows for
the period presented.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>(b)&nbsp;Management&#146;s Reports on Internal Control Over Financial Reporting</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">Our management is responsible for establishing and maintaining adequate internal control over
financial reporting. Our internal control over financial reporting is a process designed under the
supervision of our chief executive officer and chief financial officer to provide reasonable
assurance regarding the reliability of financial reporting and preparation of our financial
statements for external purposes in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">As of December&nbsp;31, 2008, management assessed the effectiveness of our internal control over
financial reporting based on the criteria for effective internal control over financial reporting
established in <I>Internal Control &#151; Integrated Framework </I>issued by the Committee of Sponsoring
Organizations of the Treadway Commission. Based on this assessment, management concluded that our
internal control over financial reporting was not effective as of December&nbsp;31, 2008, under the
criteria set forth in Internal Control Integrated Framework.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">This annual report does not include an attestation report of our registered public accounting firm
regarding internal control over financial reporting. Management&#146;s report was not subject to
attestation by our registered public accounting firm pursuant to temporary rules of the SEC that
permit us to provide only management&#146;s report in this annual report.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">In the most recent fiscal quarter, we strengthened the internal control over financial reporting.
We performed additional analysis and other post-closing procedures to ensure consolidated financial
statements are prepared in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles. We added
additional management review processes to cash reconciliation procedures and accounts payable and
accounts receivable reconciliation procedures. We believe these additional processes and
procedures have improved our internal control over financial reporting.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>(c)&nbsp;Changes in Internal Control Over Financial Reporting</B>
</DIV>


<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 6pt">There were no changes in our internal control over financial reporting that occurred during the
most recent fiscal quarter that have materially affected, or are reasonably likely to materially
affect, our internal control over financial reporting.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt"><!-- Folio -->-62-<!-- /Folio -->

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt; margin-top: 12pt"><B>Item&nbsp;15. Exhibits and Financial Statement Schedules.</B>

<!-- Begin Table Head -->
